========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t think I can say “only” with total confidence, because then I start second-guessing myself—like maybe there was a moment in my apartment or somewhere and I just didn’t label it that way. I do that a lot, I replay things and then get stuck on whether I’m remembering them right.

The buildings and the students are there, but they feel slightly unreal, like everything is normal and not normal at the same time.

So… definitely campus. Somewhere else, I can’t say definitely.

days since the lighting rig collapsed during the showcase.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 42 / teaching_assistant / graduate\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S082\n\n[Dialogue I keep trying to check my memory, like, was I in my apartment or on the bus once and felt that? Maybe briefly, but what I keep coming back to is campus.\n\nWhen I’m walking across campus, that’s when it really scares me. It’s like I’m watching everything through glass. The buildings and the students are there, and I know they’re real, but they feel slightly unreal. And I feel like I’m not fully inside my own body, like I’m watching myself move through it. That’s the clearest place it happens.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall any specific moment, even if brief, when you felt detached from yourself or that things around you felt unreal when you were not on campus, such as at home, in another city, or in a different environment?\n\nPatient: I’m trying to think of one specific time, like at home or somewhere else, but my mind keeps circling back to campus. I can’t really pull up a clear example away from campus the same way.\n\nDoctor: Can you say for sure that these feelings of unreality or being detached from yourself only happen on campus, or have you definitely felt them somewhere else, even a little bit?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I can say “only” with total confidence, because then I start second-guessing myself—like maybe there was a moment in my apartment or somewhere and I just didn’t label it that way. It feels like I’m watching everything through glass, like I’m not fully in my own body. The buildings and the students are there, but they feel slightly unreal, like everything is normal and not normal at the same time.\n\nSo… definitely campus. to minor stimuli.\n  manifestation: If a door bangs or a cart rattles in the hallway, I jump so hard my chest hurts, and it takes me several minutes to stop shaking and get back to what I was doing.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  description: required\n  manifestation: I haven’t been able to do my job normally—I walked out of a planning meeting, missed two mentoring sessions with students, and I’m avoiding parts of campus I’m supposed to use.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 6 days\n  manifestation: This has been going on for the 6 days since the lighting rig collapsed during the showcase.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 42 / teaching_assistant / graduate\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S082\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Can you recall any specific moment, even if brief, when you felt detached from yourself or that things around you felt unreal when you were not on campus, such as at home, in another I do that a lot, I replay things and then get stuck on whether I’m remembering them right.\n\nBut the thing I can say clearly is that what scares me most happens when I’m walking across campus. It feels like I’m watching everything through glass, like I’m not fully in my own body. The buildings and the students are there, but they feel slightly unreal, like everything is normal and not normal at the same time.\n\nSo… definitely campus. Somewhere else, I can’t say definitely.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any time, even briefly, when you felt detached from yourself or that things around you felt unreal while you were at home or in another familiar place?\n\nPatient: I keep trying to find an example like that, at home or somewhere familiar, and then I get distracted thinking about my apartment layout or the walk from the bus stop, and whether I felt strange there, but… I don’t have a clear one.\n\nThe one that really sticks, and scares me, is still campus. When I’m walking across it, it can feel like I’m watching everything through glass. Like I’m moving, but I’m not fully inside my own body. The buildings and students are around me, and I know they’re real, but they feel slightly unreal, like everything is a little off.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever had the feeling that things around you were unreal or that you were detached from yourself in any other situation—like when you're at home, in a store, or somewhere else—even if it was just for a few seconds?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. I keep wanting to say yes just to be helpful, but I don’t want to make something up. Like, I go to the store and I’m thinking about whether I need milk or if I forgot my keys, and then I start wondering, “Did I feel weird there too?” and it gets all muddled.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n11\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF48.1, F44.2\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, I'm Dr. [Your Name]. Could you please tell me your name and what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: My name is… I’m sorry, I’m kind of scattered. I’m here because something feels really wrong when I’m on campus. I work as a teaching assistant, and lately when I walk between buildings, it’s like I’m looking at everything through glass. The students passing by, the sidewalks, the buildings—it all feels slightly unreal, and I don’t feel fully inside my own body.\n\nIt reminds me of those museum displays behind thick glass, where you can see everything but can’t quite reach it. I know that probably sounds strange. But that’s what scares me the most.\n\nDoctor: When you say things feel unreal, does the world around you ever seem distorted, foggy, or dreamlike, like you’re living in a movie or a dream?\n\nPatient: Yes… dreamlike is pretty close, I think. Not exactly like I’m asleep, but when I’m walking across campus it’s like there’s this pane of glass between me and everything else. The buildings, the students going by, even the sidewalks all look normal, but they feel… slightly unreal.\n\nAnd I sort of feel like I’m not fully inside my own body while it’s happening. It scares me because campus is supposed to be familiar—I mean, I’ve spent so much time there, I know which doors stick and where the noisy vending machine is—and then suddenly it feels like I’m just watching it instead of being there.\n\nDoctor: Do these feelings of unreality or being detached from yourself happen only on campus, or do they also occur in other places or situations, like at home or when you're with friends?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s on campus. I mean, that’s where I really notice it, when I’m walking between buildings and there are students everywhere and I’m supposed to just be normal, going to class or office hours or whatever.\n\nIt’s like… the whole place is behind glass. The buildings and people look real, but they don’t feel real, and I don’t feel quite inside myself. I keep thinking about those display cases in museums—sorry, that’s probably not exactly what you asked—but that’s the closest thing I can compare it to. Like I’m watching it all instead of being there, and that’s the part that scares me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced these same feelings of unreality or detachment when you're not on campus, such as at home, in your car, or in other familiar places?\n\nPatient: I… I’m trying to think. It’s mainly campus. That’s where it hits me hardest, like when I’m walking across the quad or between buildings and there are students moving around me, and suddenly it feels like I’m looking at everything through glass.\n\nI know you asked about home or the car—I’m sorry, my mind keeps going back to campus. It’s just that there, the buildings and people look normal, but they feel slightly unreal, and I feel like I’m not fully in my own body. Like I’m watching myself walk instead of actually being there. That’s the part that scares me most.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced these feelings of unreality or detachment from yourself in places other than campus, like at home, in your car, or somewhere else entirely?\n\nPatient: I know you’re asking if it happens somewhere else, and I’m trying not to dodge the question… but the place I keep coming back to is campus. That’s where it really scares me.\n\nWhen I’m walking across campus, it can suddenly feel like I’m watching everything through glass. The buildings are there, the students are there, everything looks normal, but it all feels slightly unreal. And I don’t feel fully in my own body, like I’m watching myself move through it instead of actually being there.\n\nI’m sorry, I know that doesn’t really answer the home or car part clearly. It’s just… campus is where I notice it most.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had moments, even just briefly, where you felt detached from yourself or things around you felt unreal when you weren’t on campus—like when you're at home, in another city, or in a different environment?\n\nPatient: I’m… not sure I can say it happens the same way somewhere else. I keep trying to check my memory, like, was I in my apartment or on the bus once and felt that? Maybe briefly, but what I keep coming back to is campus.\n\nWhen I’m walking across campus, that’s when it really scares me. It’s like I’m watching everything through glass. The buildings and the students are there, and I know they’re real, but they feel slightly unreal. And I feel like I’m not fully inside my own body, like I’m watching myself move through it. That’s the clearest place it happens.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall any specific moment, even if brief, when you felt detached from yourself or that things around you felt unreal when you were not on campus, such as at home, in another city, or in a different environment?\n\nPatient: I’m trying to think of one specific time, like at home or somewhere else, but my mind keeps circling back to campus. Sorry, I know that’s not exactly what you asked.\n\nIt’s just… that’s the moment I can actually picture. I’ll be walking across campus, and suddenly it feels like I’m watching everything through glass. The buildings and the students are right there, and I know they’re real, but they feel kind of… off, slightly unreal. And I feel like I’m not fully in my own body, like I’m watching myself walk instead of being the one walking.\n\nThat’s the part that scares me most. I can’t really pull up a clear example away from campus the same way.\n\nDoctor: Can you say for sure that these feelings of unreality or being detached from yourself only happen on campus, or have you definitely felt them somewhere else, even a little bit?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I can say “only” with total confidence, because then I start second-guessing myself—like maybe there was a moment in my apartment or somewhere and I just didn’t label it that way. It feels like I’m watching everything through glass, like I’m not fully in my own body. The buildings and the students are there, but they feel slightly unreal, like everything is normal and not normal at the same time.\n\nSo… definitely campus. Somewhere else, I can’t say definitely.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any time, even briefly, when you felt detached from yourself or that things around you felt unreal while you were at home or in another familiar place?\n\nPatient: I keep trying to find an example like that, at home or somewhere familiar, and then I get distracted thinking about my apartment layout or the walk from the bus stop, and whether I felt strange there, but… I don’t have a clear one.\n\nThe one that really sticks, and scares me, is still campus. Like I’m watching it all instead of being there, and that’s the part that scares me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced these same feelings of unreality or detachment when you're not on campus, such as at home, in your car, or in other familiar places?\n\nPatient: I… I’m trying to think. It’s mainly campus. That’s where it hits me hardest, like when I’m walking across the quad or between buildings and there are students moving around me, and suddenly it feels like I’m looking at everything through glass.\n\nI know you asked about home or the car—I’m sorry, my mind keeps going back to campus. It’s just that there, the buildings and people look normal, but they feel slightly unreal, and I feel like I’m not fully in my own body. Like I’m watching myself walk instead of actually being there. That’s the part that scares me most.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced these feelings of unreality or detachment from yourself in places other than campus, like at home, in your car, or somewhere else entirely?\n\nPatient: I know you’re asking if it happens somewhere else, and I’m trying not to dodge the question… but the place I keep coming back to is campus. That’s where it really scares me.\n\nWhen I’m walking across campus, it can suddenly feel like I’m watching everything through glass. The buildings are there, the students are there, everything looks normal, but it all feels slightly unreal. And I don’t feel fully in my own body, like I’m watching myself move through it instead of actually being there.\n\nI’m sorry, I know that doesn’t really answer the home or car part clearly. It’s just… campus is where I notice it most.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had moments, even just briefly, where you felt detached from yourself or things around you felt unreal when you weren’t on campus—like when you're at home, in another city, or in a different environment?\n\nPatient: I’m… not sure I can say it happens the same way somewhere else. I keep trying to check my memory, like, was I in my apartment or on the bus once and felt that? Maybe briefly, but what I keep coming back to is campus.\n\nWhen I’m walking across campus, that’s when it really scares me. It’s like I’m watching everything through glass. The buildings and the students are there, and I know they’re real, but they feel slightly unreal. And I feel like I’m not fully inside my own body, like I’m watching myself move through it. That’s the clearest place it happens.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall any specific moment, even if brief, when you felt detached from yourself or that things around you felt unreal when you were not on campus, such as at home, in another city, or in a different environment?\n\nPatient: I’m trying to think of one specific time, like at home or somewhere else, but my mind keeps circling back to campus. Sorry, I know that’s not exactly what you asked.\n\nIt’s just… that’s the moment I can actually picture. I’ll be walking across campus, and suddenly it feels like I’m watching everything through glass. The buildings and the students are right there, and I know they’re real, but they feel kind of… off, slightly unreal. And I feel like I’m not fully in my own body, like I’m watching myself walk instead of being the one walking.\n\nThat’s the part that scares me most. I can’t really pull up a clear example away from campus the same way.\n\nDoctor: Can you say for sure that these feelings of unreality or being detached from yourself only happen on campus, or have you definitely felt them somewhere else, even a little bit?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I can say “only” with total confidence, because then I start second-guessing myself—like maybe there was a moment in my apartment or somewhere and I just didn’t label it that way. I do that a lot, I replay things and then get stuck on whether I’m remembering them right.\n\nBut the thing I can say clearly is that what scares me most happens when I’m walking across campus. It feels like I’m watching everything through glass, like I’m not fully in my own body. The buildings and the students are there, but they feel slightly unreal, like everything is normal and not normal at the same time.\n\nSo… definitely campus. Somewhere else, I can’t say definitely.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any time, even briefly, when you felt detached from yourself or that things around you felt unreal while you were at home or in another familiar place?\n\nPatient: I keep trying to find an example like that, at home or somewhere familiar, and then I get distracted thinking about my apartment layout or the walk from the bus stop, and whether I felt strange there, but… I don’t have a clear one.\n\nThe one that really sticks, and scares me, is still campus. When I’m walking across it, it can feel like I’m watching everything through glass. Like I’m moving, but I’m not fully inside my own body. The buildings and students are around me, and I know they’re real, but they feel slightly unreal, like everything is a little off.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had the feeling that things around you were unreal or that you were detached from yourself in any other situation—like when you're at home, in a store, or somewhere else—even if it was just for a few seconds?\n\nPatient: I keep wanting to say yes just to be helpful, but I don’t want to make something up. Like, I go to the store and I’m thinking about whether I need milk or if I forgot my keys, and then I start wondering, “Did I feel weird there too?” and it gets all muddled.\n\nThe thing I can really say is the campus thing. That’s the one that scares me. When I’m walking across campus, it feels like I’m seeing everything through glass, like I’m not fully in my body. The buildings and students are there, I know they are, but they feel slightly unreal, like everything is just… off.\n\nSo other places, I’m not sure. But on campus, yes, definitely.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any time—maybe in your apartment, a store, or another place—when you suddenly felt like things around you were unreal or that you were detached from yourself, even for just a few seconds?\n\nPatient: I’m trying to think of a specific time like that in my apartment or a store, but my mind keeps jumping around—like I remember standing in the cereal aisle once and thinking the lighting was awful, and then I start wondering if that counts, but I don’t really know.\n\nThe clearest thing is still campus. That’s the part that scares me. When I’m walking across campus, it can feel like I’m watching everything through glass, like I’m there but not fully in my own body. I just… I keep thinking of home, like standing in my kitchen or walking to the mailbox, and then I start thinking about whether I left dishes in the sink, or the weird humming sound the fridge makes, and I lose the thread.\n\nWhat I can say clearly is that the really frightening feeling happens on campus. When I walk across it, it’s like I’m looking through glass, like I’m moving but not completely inside myself. The students and buildings are there, I know they’re real, but they feel kind of unreal, just slightly wrong. That’s the one I’m sure about.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. It scares me because campus is supposed to be familiar—I mean, I’ve spent so much time there, I know which doors stick and where the noisy vending machine is—and then suddenly it feels like I’m just watching it instead of being there.\n\nDoctor: Do these feelings of unreality or being detached from yourself happen only on campus, or do they also occur in other places or situations, like at home or when you're with friends?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s on campus. I mean, that’s where I really notice it, when I’m walking between buildings and there are students everywhere and I’m supposed to just be normal, going to class or office hours or whatever.\n\nIt’s like… the whole place is behind glass. The buildings and people look real, but they don’t feel real, and I don’t feel quite inside myself. I keep thinking about those display cases in museums—sorry, that’s probably not exactly what you asked—but that’s the closest thing I can compare it to. Like I’m watching it all instead of being there, and that’s the part that scares me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced these same feelings of unreality or detachment when you're not on campus, such as at home, in your car, or in other familiar places?\n\nPatient: I… I’m trying to think. It’s mainly campus. That’s where it hits me hardest, like when I’m walking across the quad or between buildings and there are students moving around me, and suddenly it feels like I’m looking at everything through glass.\n\nI know you asked about home or the car—I’m sorry, my mind keeps going back to campus. It’s just that there, the buildings and people look normal, but they feel slightly unreal, and I feel like I’m not fully in my own body. Like I’m watching myself walk instead of actually being there. That’s the part that scares me most.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced these feelings of unreality or detachment from yourself in places other than campus, like at home, in your car, or somewhere else entirely?\n\nPatient: I know you’re asking if it happens somewhere else, and I’m trying not to dodge the question… but the place I keep coming back to is campus. That’s where it really scares me.\n\nWhen I’m walking across campus, it can suddenly feel like I’m watching everything through glass. The buildings are there, the students are there, everything looks normal, but it all feels slightly unreal. And I don’t feel fully in my own body, like I’m watching myself move through it instead of actually being there.\n\nI’m sorry, I know that doesn’t really answer the home or car part clearly. It’s just… campus is where I notice it most.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had moments, even just briefly, where you felt detached from yourself or things around you felt unreal when you weren’t on campus—like when you're at home, in another city, or in a different environment?\n\nPatient: I’m… not sure I can say it happens the same way somewhere else. I keep trying to check my memory, like, was I in my apartment or on the bus once and felt that? Maybe briefly, but what I keep coming back to is campus.\n\nWhen I’m walking across campus, that’s when it really scares me. It’s like I’m watching everything through glass. The buildings and the students are there, and I know they’re real, but they feel slightly unreal. And I feel like I’m not fully inside my own body, like I’m watching myself move through it. That’s the clearest place it happens.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall any specific moment, even if brief, when you felt detached from yourself or that things around you felt unreal when you were not on campus, such as at home, in another city, or in a different environment?\n\nPatient: I’m trying to think of one specific time, like at home or somewhere else, but my mind keeps circling back to campus. Sorry, I know that’s not exactly what you asked.\n\nIt’s just… that’s the moment I can actually picture. I’ll be walking across campus, and suddenly it feels like I’m watching everything through glass. The buildings and the students are right there, and I know they’re real, but they feel kind of… off, slightly unreal. And I feel like I’m not fully in my own body, like I’m watching myself walk instead of being the one walking.\n\nThat’s the part that scares me most. I can’t really pull up a clear example away from campus the same way.\n\nDoctor: Can you say for sure that these feelings of unreality or being detached from yourself only happen on campus, or have you definitely felt them somewhere else, even a little bit?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I can say “only” with total confidence, because then I start second-guessing myself—like maybe there was a moment in my apartment or somewhere and I just didn’t label it that way. I do that a lot, I replay things and then get stuck on whether I’m remembering them right.\n\nBut the thing I can say clearly is that what scares me most happens when I’m walking across campus. It feels like I’m watching everything through glass, like I’m not fully in my own body. The buildings and the students are there, but they feel slightly unreal, like everything is normal and not normal at the same time.\n\nSo… definitely campus. Somewhere else, I can’t say definitely.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any time, even briefly, when you felt detached from yourself or that things around you felt unreal while you were at home or in another familiar place?\n\nPatient: I keep trying to find an example like that, at home or somewhere familiar, and then I get distracted thinking about my apartment layout or the walk from the bus stop, and whether I felt strange there, but… I don’t have a clear one.\n\nThe one that really sticks, and scares me, is still campus. When I’m walking across it, it can feel like I’m watching everything through glass. Like I’m moving, but I’m not fully inside my own body. The buildings and students are around me, and I know they’re real, but they feel slightly unreal, like everything is a little off.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had the feeling that things around you were unreal or that you were detached from yourself in any other situation—like when you're at home, in a store, or somewhere else—even if it was just for a few seconds?\n\nPatient: I keep wanting to say yes just to be helpful, but I don’t want to make something up. Like, I go to the store and I’m thinking about whether I need milk or if I forgot my keys, and then I start wondering, “Did I feel weird there too?” and it gets all muddled.\n\nThe thing I can really say is the campus thing. That’s the one that scares me. When I’m walking across campus, it feels like I’m seeing everything through glass, like I’m not fully in my body. The buildings and students are there, I know they are, but they feel slightly unreal, like everything is just… off.\n\nSo other places, I’m not sure. But on campus, yes, definitely.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any time—maybe in your apartment, a store, or another place—when you suddenly felt like things around you were unreal or that you were detached from yourself, even for just a few seconds?\n\nPatient: I’m trying to think of a specific time like that in my apartment or a store, but my mind keeps jumping around—like I remember standing in the cereal aisle once and thinking the lighting was awful, and then I start wondering if that counts, but I don’t really know.\n\nThe clearest thing is still campus. That’s the part that scares me. When I’m walking across campus, it can feel like I’m watching everything through glass, like I’m there but not fully in my own body. I just… I keep thinking of home, like standing in my kitchen or walking to the mailbox, and then I start thinking about whether I left dishes in the sink, or the weird humming sound the fridge makes, and I lose the thread.\n\nWhat I can say clearly is that the really frightening feeling happens on campus. When I walk across it, it’s like I’m looking through glass, like I’m moving but not completely inside myself. The students and buildings are there, I know they’re real, but they feel kind of unreal, just slightly wrong. That’s the one I’m sure about.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any moment—no matter how brief or mild—when you felt detached from yourself or that the world felt unreal while you were somewhere other than campus, such as at home, in a store, or while riding in a car?\n\nPatient: I’m trying to think of one that wasn’t campus, like in the car or at home, but then I start thinking about the route I take or whether I had groceries in the trunk, and I kind of lose the actual feeling you’re asking about.\n\nThe thing I can really pin down is still campus. That’s what scares me most. When I walk across campus, it feels like I’m watching everything through glass, like I’m not fully in my own body. The buildings and students are right there, I know that, but they feel slightly unreal somehow. Like everything is normal but also not normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had even a brief moment where you felt detached from yourself or that the world felt unreal while you were doing something routine, like cooking, driving, or watching TV at home?\n\nPatient: I’m trying to picture that, like cooking or driving or just sitting on the couch with the TV on, and I keep getting distracted by little things—like whether I had something on the stove, or what show was playing, and then I’m not sure if I’m answering you right.\n\nThe thing I keep coming back to is campus. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I know you’re asking about cooking or driving or watching TV, and I keep trying to find an example there, but then I start thinking about the pan on the stove or what channel was on, and I sort of get lost in the details.

Like I’m moving, but I’m not fully in my own body. The buildings and the students are all there, I know they’re real, but they seem slightly unreal, like everything is just a little off.

Could you please tell me your name and what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: My name is… I’m sorry, I’m kind of scattered. I’m here because something feels really wrong when I’m on campus. I work as a teaching assistant, and lately when I walk between buildings, it’s like I’m looking at everything through glass. The students passing by, the sidewalks, the buildings—it all feels slightly unreal, and I don’t feel fully inside my own body.\n\nIt reminds me of those museum displays behind thick glass, where you can see everything but can’t quite reach it. I know that probably sounds strange. But that’s what scares me the most.\n\nDoctor: When you say things feel unreal, does the world around you ever seem distorted, foggy, or dreamlike, like you’re living in a movie or a dream?\n\nPatient: Yes… dreamlike is pretty close, I think. Not exactly like I’m asleep, but when I’m walking across campus it’s like there’s this pane of glass between me and everything else. The buildings, the students going by, even the sidewalks all look normal, but they feel… slightly unreal.\n\nAnd I sort of feel like I’m not fully inside my own body while it’s happening. It scares me because campus is supposed to be familiar—I mean, I’ve spent so much time there, I know which doors stick and where the noisy vending machine is—and then suddenly it feels like I’m just watching it instead of being there.\n\nDoctor: Do these feelings of unreality or being detached from yourself happen only on campus, or do they also occur in other places or situations, like at home or when you're with friends?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s on campus. I mean, that’s where I really notice it, when I’m walking between buildings and there are students everywhere and I’m supposed to just be normal, going to class or office hours or whatever.\n\nIt’s like… the whole place is behind glass. The buildings and people look real, but they don’t feel real, and I don’t feel quite inside myself. I keep thinking about those display cases in museums—sorry, that’s probably not exactly what you asked—but that’s the closest thing I can compare it to. Like I’m watching it all instead of being there, and that’s the part that scares me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced these same feelings of unreality or detachment when you're not on campus, such as at home, in your car, or in other familiar places?\n\nPatient: I… I’m trying to think. It’s mainly campus. That’s where it hits me hardest, like when I’m walking across the quad or between buildings and there are students moving around me, and suddenly it feels like I’m looking at everything through glass.\n\nI know you asked about home or the car—I’m sorry, my mind keeps going back to campus. It’s just that there, the buildings and people look normal, but they feel slightly unreal, and I feel like I’m not fully in my own body. Like I’m watching myself walk instead of actually being there. That’s the part that scares me most.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced these feelings of unreality or detachment from yourself in places other than campus, like at home, in your car, or somewhere else entirely?\n\nPatient: I know you’re asking if it happens somewhere else, and I’m trying not to dodge the question… but the place I keep coming back to is campus. That’s where it really scares me.\n\nWhen I’m walking across campus, it can suddenly feel like I’m watching everything through glass. The buildings are there, the students are there, everything looks normal, but it all feels slightly unreal. And I don’t feel fully in my own body, like I’m watching myself move through it instead of actually being there.\n\nI’m sorry, I know that doesn’t really answer the home or car part clearly. It’s just… campus is where I notice it most.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had moments, even just briefly, where you felt detached from yourself or things around you felt unreal when you weren’t on campus—like when you're at home, in another city, or in a different environment?\n\nPatient: I’m… not sure I can say it happens the same way somewhere else. I keep trying to check my memory, like, was I in my apartment or on the bus once and felt that? Maybe briefly, but what I keep coming back to is campus.\n\nWhen I’m walking across campus, that’s when it really scares me. It’s like I’m watching everything through glass. The buildings and the students are there, and I know they’re real, but they feel slightly unreal. And I feel like I’m not fully inside my own body, like I’m watching myself move through it. That’s the clearest place it happens.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall any specific moment, even if brief, when you felt detached from yourself or that things around you felt unreal when you were not on campus, such as at home, in another city, or in a different environment?\n\nPatient: I’m trying to think of one specific time, like at home or somewhere else, but my mind keeps circling back to campus. Sorry, I know that’s not exactly what you asked.\n\nIt’s just… that’s the moment I can actually picture. I’ll be walking across campus, and suddenly it feels like I’m watching everything through glass. The buildings and the students are right there, and I know they’re real, but they feel kind of… off, slightly unreal. And I feel like I’m not fully in my own body, like I’m watching myself walk instead of being the one walking.\n\nThat’s the part that scares me most. I can’t really pull up a clear example away from campus the same way.\n\nDoctor: Can you say for sure that these feelings of unreality or being detached from yourself only happen on campus, or have you definitely felt them somewhere else, even a little bit?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I can say “only” with total confidence, because then I start second-guessing myself—like maybe there was a moment in my apartment or somewhere and I just didn’t label it that way. I do that a lot, I replay things and then get stuck on whether I’m remembering them right.\n\nBut the thing I can say clearly is that what scares me most happens when I’m walking across campus. It feels like I’m watching everything through glass, like I’m not fully in my own body. The buildings and the students are there, but they feel slightly unreal, like everything is normal and not normal at the same time.\n\nSo… definitely campus. Somewhere else, I can’t say definitely.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any time, even briefly, when you felt detached from yourself or that things around you felt unreal while you were at home or in another familiar place?\n\nPatient: I keep trying to find an example like that, at home or somewhere familiar, and then I get distracted thinking about my apartment layout or the walk from the bus stop, and whether I felt strange there, but… I don’t have a clear one.\n\nThe one that really sticks, and scares me, is still campus. When I’m walking across it, it can feel like I’m watching everything through glass. Like I’m moving, but I’m not fully inside my own body. The buildings and students are around me, and I know they’re real, but they feel slightly unreal, like everything is a little off.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had the feeling that things around you were unreal or that you were detached from yourself in any other situation—like when you're at home, in a store, or somewhere else—even if it was just for a few seconds?\n\nPatient: I keep wanting to say yes just to be helpful, but I don’t want to make something up. Like, I go to the store and I’m thinking about whether I need milk or if I forgot my keys, and then I start wondering, “Did I feel weird there too?” and it gets all muddled.\n\nThe thing I can really say is the campus thing. That’s the one that scares me. When I’m walking across campus, it feels like I’m seeing everything through glass, like I’m not fully in my body. The buildings and students are there, I know they are, but they feel slightly unreal, like everything is just… off.\n\nSo other places, I’m not sure. But on campus, yes, definitely.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any time—maybe in your apartment, a store, or another place—when you suddenly felt like things around you were unreal or that you were detached from yourself, even for just a few seconds?\n\nPatient: I’m trying to think of a specific time like that in my apartment or a store, but my mind keeps jumping around—like I remember standing in the cereal aisle once and thinking the lighting was awful, and then I start wondering if that counts, but I don’t really know.\n\nThe clearest thing is still campus. That’s the part that scares me. When I’m walking across campus, it can feel like I’m watching everything through glass, like I’m there but not fully in my own body. It’s just that there, the buildings and people look normal, but they feel slightly unreal, and I feel like I’m not fully in my own body. Like I’m watching myself walk instead of actually being there. That’s the part that scares me most.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced these feelings of unreality or detachment from yourself in places other than campus, like at home, in your car, or somewhere else entirely?\n\nPatient: I know you’re asking if it happens somewhere else, and I’m trying not to dodge the question… but the place I keep coming back to is campus. That’s where it really scares me.\n\nWhen I’m walking across campus, it can suddenly feel like I’m watching everything through glass. The buildings are there, the students are there, everything looks normal, but it all feels slightly unreal. And I don’t feel fully in my own body, like I’m watching myself move through it instead of actually being there.\n\nI’m sorry, I know that doesn’t really answer the home or car part clearly. It’s just… campus is where I notice it most.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had moments, even just briefly, where you felt detached from yourself or things around you felt unreal when you weren’t on campus—like when you're at home, in another city, or in a different environment?\n\nPatient: I’m… not sure I can say it happens the same way somewhere else. I keep trying to check my memory, like, was I in my apartment or on the bus once and felt that? Maybe briefly, but what I keep coming back to is campus.\n\nWhen I’m walking across campus, that’s when it really scares me. It’s like I’m watching everything through glass. The buildings and the students are there, and I know they’re real, but they feel slightly unreal. And I feel like I’m not fully inside my own body, like I’m watching myself move through it. That’s the clearest place it happens.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall any specific moment, even if brief, when you felt detached from yourself or that things around you felt unreal when you were not on campus, such as at home, in another city, or in a different environment?\n\nPatient: I’m trying to think of one specific time, like at home or somewhere else, but my mind keeps circling back to campus. Sorry, I know that’s not exactly what you asked.\n\nIt’s just… that’s the moment I can actually picture. I’ll be walking across campus, and suddenly it feels like I’m watching everything through glass. The buildings and the students are right there, and I know they’re real, but they feel kind of… off, slightly unreal. And I feel like I’m not fully in my own body, like I’m watching myself walk instead of being the one walking.\n\nThat’s the part that scares me most. I can’t really pull up a clear example away from campus the same way.\n\nDoctor: Can you say for sure that these feelings of unreality or being detached from yourself only happen on campus, or have you definitely felt them somewhere else, even a little bit?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I can say “only” with total confidence, because then I start second-guessing myself—like maybe there was a moment in my apartment or somewhere and I just didn’t label it that way. I do that a lot, I replay things and then get stuck on whether I’m remembering them right.\n\nBut the thing I can say clearly is that what scares me most happens when I’m walking across campus. It feels like I’m watching everything through glass, like I’m not fully in my own body. The buildings and the students are there, but they feel slightly unreal, like everything is normal and not normal at the same time.\n\nSo… definitely campus. Somewhere else, I can’t say definitely.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any time, even briefly, when you felt detached from yourself or that things around you felt unreal while you were at home or in another familiar place?\n\nPatient: I keep trying to find an example like that, at home or somewhere familiar, and then I get distracted thinking about my apartment layout or the walk from the bus stop, and whether I felt strange there, but… I don’t have a clear one.\n\nThe one that really sticks, and scares me, is still campus. When I’m walking across it, it can feel like I’m watching everything through glass. Like I’m moving, but I’m not fully inside my own body. The buildings and students are around me, and I know they’re real, but they feel slightly unreal, like everything is a little off.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had the feeling that things around you were unreal or that you were detached from yourself in any other situation—like when you're at home, in a store, or somewhere else—even if it was just for a few seconds?\n\nPatient: I keep wanting to say yes just to be helpful, but I don’t want to make something up. Like, I go to the store and I’m thinking about whether I need milk or if I forgot my keys, and then I start wondering, “Did I feel weird there too?” and it gets all muddled.\n\nThe thing I can really say is the campus thing. That’s the one that scares me. When I’m walking across campus, it feels like I’m seeing everything through glass, like I’m not fully in my body. The buildings and students are there, I know they are, but they feel slightly unreal, like everything is just… off.\n\nSo other places, I’m not sure. But on campus, yes, definitely.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any time—maybe in your apartment, a store, or another place—when you suddenly felt like things around you were unreal or that you were detached from yourself, even for just a few seconds?\n\nPatient: I’m trying to think of a specific time like that in my apartment or a store, but my mind keeps jumping around—like I remember standing in the cereal aisle once and thinking the lighting was awful, and then I start wondering if that counts, but I don’t really know.\n\nThe clearest thing is still campus. That’s the part that scares me. When I’m walking across campus, it can feel like I’m watching everything through glass, like I’m there but not fully in my own body. I can see the buildings and the students, and I know they’re real, but they feel slightly unreal, like everything is just off somehow.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you were watching yourself from the outside or that the world felt unreal, even for just a moment, when you weren’t on campus—like when you’re at home, in a store, or somewhere else familiar?\n\nPatient: I know you’re asking about when I’m not on campus, and I’m trying not to dodge it. I just… I keep thinking of home, like standing in my kitchen or walking to the mailbox, and then I start thinking about whether I left dishes in the sink, or the weird humming sound the fridge makes, and I lose the thread.\n\nWhat I can say clearly is that the really frightening feeling happens on campus. When I walk across it, it’s like I’m looking through glass, like I’m moving but not completely inside myself. The students and buildings are there, I know they’re real, but they feel kind of unreal, just slightly wrong. That’s the one I’m sure about.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any moment—no matter how brief or mild—when you felt detached from yourself or that the world felt unreal while you were somewhere other than campus, such as at home, in a store, or while riding in a car?\n\nPatient: I’m trying to think of one that wasn’t campus, like in the car or at home, but then I start thinking about the route I take or whether I had groceries in the trunk, and I kind of lose the actual feeling you’re asking about.\n\nThe thing I can really pin down is still campus. That’s what scares me most. When I walk across campus, it feels like I’m watching everything through glass, like I’m not fully in my own body. The buildings and students are right there, I know that, but they feel slightly unreal somehow. Like everything is normal but also not normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had even a brief moment where you felt detached from yourself or that the world felt unreal while you were doing something routine, like cooking, driving, or watching TV at home?\n\nPatient: I’m trying to picture that, like cooking or driving or just sitting on the couch with the TV on, and I keep getting distracted by little things—like whether I had something on the stove, or what show was playing, and then I’m not sure if I’m answering you right.\n\nThe thing I keep coming back to is campus. It’s like I’m seeing everything through glass, like I’m there but not fully inside myself. The buildings, the students passing by, they’re all real, I know that, but they feel slightly unreal, like everything is normal and wrong at the same time.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any time, even a brief or mild moment, when you felt detached from yourself or that things around you felt unreal while you were somewhere other than campus, such as at home, in a store, or while doing something routine like cooking or driving?\n\nPatient: I keep trying to think of home or the store, like standing in an aisle trying to remember what I came in for, or cooking and worrying I left something on too long, but I’m not sure that’s the same thing. The buildings and the students are right there, and I know they’re real, but they feel slightly unreal, like everything is off somehow.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever had even a fleeting moment where things around you felt unreal or you felt detached from yourself in any situation—like at home, in a store, or while doing something routine—even if it didn’t scare you or last very long?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. I’m trying to answer that exactly, and I keep getting stuck on the examples—home, store, routine things—because then I start thinking about, like, whether I was tired or distracted or just zoning out.

  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "/no_think\nYou are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. Could you please tell me your name and what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: My name is… I’m sorry, I’m kind of scattered. I’m here because something feels really wrong when I’m on campus. I work as a teaching assistant, and lately when I walk between buildings, it’s like I’m looking at everything through glass. The students passing by, the sidewalks, the buildings—it all feels slightly unreal, and I don’t feel fully inside my own body.\n\nIt reminds me of those museum displays behind thick glass, where you can see everything but can’t quite reach it. I know that probably sounds strange. But that’s what scares me the most.\n\nDoctor: When you say things feel unreal, does the world around you ever seem distorted, foggy, or dreamlike, like you’re living in a movie or a dream?\n\nPatient: Yes… dreamlike is pretty close, I think. Not exactly like I’m asleep, but when I’m walking across campus it’s like there’s this pane of glass between me and everything else. The buildings, the students going by, even the sidewalks all look normal, but they feel… slightly unreal.\n\nAnd I sort of feel like I’m not fully inside my own body while it’s happening. It scares me because campus is supposed to be familiar—I mean, I’ve spent so much time there, I know which doors stick and where the noisy vending machine is—and then suddenly it feels like I’m just watching it instead of being there.\n\nDoctor: Do these feelings of unreality or being detached from yourself happen only on campus, or do they also occur in other places or situations, like at home or when you're with friends?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s on campus. I mean, that’s where I really notice it, when I’m walking between buildings and there are students everywhere and I’m supposed to just be normal, going to class or office hours or whatever.\n\nIt’s like… the whole place is behind glass. The buildings and people look real, but they don’t feel real, and I don’t feel quite inside myself. I keep thinking about those display cases in museums—sorry, that’s probably not exactly what you asked—but that’s the closest thing I can compare it to. Like I’m watching it all instead of being there, and that’s the part that scares me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced these same feelings of unreality or detachment when you're not on campus, such as at home, in your car, or in other familiar places?\n\nPatient: I… I’m trying to think. It’s mainly campus. That’s where it hits me hardest, like when I’m walking across the quad or between buildings and there are students moving around me, and suddenly it feels like I’m looking at everything through glass.\n\nI know you asked about home or the car—I’m sorry, my mind keeps going back to campus. It’s just that there, the buildings and people look normal, but they feel slightly unreal, and I feel like I’m not fully in my own body. Like I’m watching myself walk instead of actually being there. That’s the part that scares me most.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced these feelings of unreality or detachment from yourself in places other than campus, like at home, in your car, or somewhere else entirely?\n\nPatient: I know you’re asking if it happens somewhere else, and I’m trying not to dodge the question… but the place I keep coming back to is campus. That’s where it really scares me.\n\nWhen I’m walking across campus, it can suddenly feel like I’m watching everything through glass. The buildings are there, the students are there, everything looks normal, but it all feels slightly unreal. And I don’t feel fully in my own body, like I’m watching myself move through it instead of actually being there.\n\nI’m sorry, I know that doesn’t really answer the home or car part clearly. It’s just… campus is where I notice it most.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had moments, even just briefly, where you felt detached from yourself or things around you felt unreal when you weren’t on campus—like when you're at home, in another city, or in a different environment?\n\nPatient: I’m… not sure I can say it happens the same way somewhere else. I keep trying to check my memory, like, was I in my apartment or on the bus once and felt that? The buildings and the students are there, and I know they’re real, but they feel slightly unreal. And I feel like I’m not fully inside my own body, like I’m watching myself move through it. That’s the clearest place it happens.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall any specific moment, even if brief, when you felt detached from yourself or that things around you felt unreal when you were not on campus, such as at home, in another city, or in a different environment?\n\nPatient: I’m trying to think of one specific time, like at home or somewhere else, but my mind keeps circling back to campus. Sorry, I know that’s not exactly what you asked.\n\nIt’s just… that’s the moment I can actually picture. I’ll be walking across campus, and suddenly it feels like I’m watching everything through glass. The buildings and the students are right there, and I know they’re real, but they feel kind of… off, slightly unreal. And I feel like I’m not fully in my own body, like I’m watching myself walk instead of being the one walking.\n\nThat’s the part that scares me most. I can’t really pull up a clear example away from campus the same way.\n\nDoctor: Can you say for sure that these feelings of unreality or being detached from yourself only happen on campus, or have you definitely felt them somewhere else, even a little bit?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I can say “only” with total confidence, because then I start second-guessing myself—like maybe there was a moment in my apartment or somewhere and I just didn’t label it that way. I do that a lot, I replay things and then get stuck on whether I’m remembering them right.\n\nBut the thing I can say clearly is that what scares me most happens when I’m walking across campus. It feels like I’m watching everything through glass, like I’m not fully in my own body. The buildings and the students are there, but they feel slightly unreal, like everything is normal and not normal at the same time.\n\nSo… definitely campus. Somewhere else, I can’t say definitely.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any time, even briefly, when you felt detached from yourself or that things around you felt unreal while you were at home or in another familiar place?\n\nPatient: I keep trying to find an example like that, at home or somewhere familiar, and then I get distracted thinking about my apartment layout or the walk from the bus stop, and whether I felt strange there, but… I don’t have a clear one.\n\nThe one that really sticks, and scares me, is still campus. When I’m walking across it, it can feel like I’m watching everything through glass. Like I’m moving, but I’m not fully inside my own body. The buildings and students are around me, and I know they’re real, but they feel slightly unreal, like everything is a little off.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had the feeling that things around you were unreal or that you were detached from yourself in any other situation—like when you're at home, in a store, or somewhere else—even if it was just for a few seconds?\n\nPatient: I keep wanting to say yes just to be helpful, but I don’t want to make something up. Like, I go to the store and I’m thinking about whether I need milk or if I forgot my keys, and then I start wondering, “Did I feel weird there too?” and it gets all muddled.\n\nThe thing I can really say is the campus thing. That’s the one that scares me. When I’m walking across campus, it feels like I’m seeing everything through glass, like I’m not fully in my body. The buildings and students are there, I know they are, but they feel slightly unreal, like everything is just… off.\n\nSo other places, I’m not sure. But on campus, yes, definitely.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any time—maybe in your apartment, a store, or another place—when you suddenly felt like things around you were unreal or that you were detached from yourself, even for just a few seconds?\n\nPatient: I’m trying to think of a specific time like that in my apartment or a store, but my mind keeps jumping around—like I remember standing in the cereal aisle once and thinking the lighting was awful, and then I start wondering if that counts, but I don’t really know.\n\nThe clearest thing is still campus. That’s the part that scares me. When I’m walking across campus, it can feel like I’m watching everything through glass, like I’m there but not fully in my own body. I can see the buildings and the students, and I know they’re real, but they feel slightly unreal, like everything is just off somehow.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you were watching yourself from the outside or that the world felt unreal, even for just a moment, when you weren’t on campus—like when you’re at home, in a store, or somewhere else familiar?\n\nPatient: I know you’re asking about when I’m not on campus, and I’m trying not to dodge it. I just… I keep thinking of home, like standing in my kitchen or walking to the mailbox, and then I start thinking about whether I left dishes in the sink, or the weird humming sound the fridge makes, and I lose the thread.\n\nWhat I can say clearly is that the really frightening feeling happens on campus. When I walk across it, it’s like I’m looking through glass, like I’m moving but not completely inside myself. The students and buildings are there, I know they’re real, but they feel kind of unreal, just slightly wrong. That’s the one I’m sure about.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any moment—no matter how brief or mild—when you felt detached from yourself or that the world felt unreal while you were somewhere other than campus, such as at home, in a store, or while riding in a car?\n\nPatient: I’m trying to think of one that wasn’t campus, like in the car or at home, but then I start thinking about the route I take or whether I had groceries in the trunk, and I kind of lose the actual feeling you’re asking about.\n\nThe thing I can really pin down is still campus. Sorry, I know that’s not exactly what you asked.\n\nIt’s just… that’s the moment I can actually picture. I do that a lot, I replay things and then get stuck on whether I’m remembering them right.\n\nBut the thing I can say clearly is that what scares me most happens when I’m walking across campus. I can’t really pull up a clear example away from campus the same way.\n\nDoctor: Can you say for sure that these feelings of unreality or being detached from yourself only happen on campus, or have you definitely felt them somewhere else, even a little bit?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I can say “only” with total confidence, because then I start second-guessing myself—like maybe there was a moment in my apartment or somewhere and I just didn’t label it that way. I do that a lot, I replay things and then get stuck on whether I’m remembering them right.\n\nBut the thing I can say clearly is that what scares me most happens when I’m walking across campus. It feels like I’m watching everything through glass, like I’m not fully in my own body. The buildings and the students are there, but they feel slightly unreal, like everything is normal and not normal at the same time.\n\nSo… definitely campus. When I’m walking across it, it can feel like I’m watching everything through glass. Like I’m moving, but I’m not fully inside my own body. The buildings and students are around me, and I know they’re real, but they feel slightly unreal, like everything is a little off.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had the feeling that things around you were unreal or that you were detached from yourself in any other situation—like when you're at home, in a store, or somewhere else—even if it was just for a few seconds?\n\nPatient: I keep wanting to say yes just to be helpful, but I don’t want to make something up. I just… I keep thinking of home, like standing in my kitchen or walking to the mailbox, and then I start thinking about whether I left dishes in the sink, or the weird humming sound the fridge makes, and I lose the thread.\n\nWhat I can say clearly is that the really frightening feeling happens on campus. When I walk across it, it’s like I’m looking through glass, like I’m moving but not completely inside myself. The buildings and students are right there, I know that, but they feel slightly unreal somehow.
